▶ NF-C0e — PROJECT THE COMMONLY-CAPTURED SCORING TERMS (turn "captured" into "applied", where the signal is real)   [Model · 🧭 OPUS · 🔬 §0.5 where a predictive model is selected · extends NF1.6's K+DST work · from the operator ask 2026-08-01]
✅✅ **DONE 2026-08-04 — 4 PRs (#573 story + #575/#577/#578 follow-ups) all MERGED to dev + operator steps executed + verified in prod. Fast 5359 / slow 26; new guards RED-proven. best_alpha-equivalent (a projection product, no edge claim).** 15 terms GRADUATED (the nine `dst_ya_g_*` yards-allowed expected-games buckets + the season-total/per-game pair + `def_forced_fumble` + `two_pt` + the three long-TD bonuses `pass/rush/rec_td_40p`); 4 LEFT CAPTURED WITH EVIDENCE (`pat_missed` 8/16 folds = availability-not-evidence, `fum`, `st_player_td`, `fumble_rec_td`); 6 NO-SUBSTRATE (recorded untestable, not untested). Operator's ESPN league applied/captured 23/16 → **35/4**.
🚨 **THE HEADLINE WASN'T IN THE STORY — A LIVE ESPN SCORING OUTAGE, found + fixed (#573).** `espn.py` mapped ESPN stat ids 3/24/42/72 onto SLEEPER's platform keys (`pass_yd/rush_yd/rec_yd/fum_lost`) instead of the CANONICAL keys (`pass_yds/rush_yds/rec_yds/fumbles_lost`); unrecognised keys pass through as "CAPTURED" by design ⇒ **every ESPN-imported league had been scoring ZERO passing/rushing/receiving yardage since ESPN import shipped** (boards ranked almost entirely on TDs + receptions). Fixed the map; that one fix moved the operator's league 23/16→27/12. ⚠️ **WHY IT SURVIVED (guard-hygiene canon): `test_core_scoring_is_applied` asserted `per_stat["pass_yd"]==0.04` — reading the value back under the key the ADAPTER WROTE. A test that reads a value back under the key the code writes can NEVER catch a wrong key** — it's a restatement of the code, not a test of it (the INC-38/39 vacuous-guard family). Cure: assert the CANONICAL contract key + the APPLIED verdict from the REAL engine + a mechanical cross-adapter agreement guard. ⚠️ Also — **`|weight|` IS NOT IMPACT: `|weight| × the stat's VOLUME` is.** NF-C0d telemetry ranked `pass_yd` DEAD LAST of 38 rows on frequency×|weight| (0.04) while it was the single most consequential term in the corpus. Correct the telemetry-ranking read accordingly (NF-C0d's flag, now proven).
⭐ **CARRY-FORWARD — the tier construction:** each `dst_ya_g_*` bucket is the EXPECTED NUMBER OF GAMES in that band, so a league's per-game tier table scores as `Σ tier_points × expected_games` — LINEAR, i.e. ANY hand-entered tier scheme is applied EXACTLY by the existing client scorer with NO engine change (the property that made NF1.6's `dst_pa_g_*` work). Two data findings to preserve: (1) **yards allowed must be NET of sack yardage** — nflverse `total_yards` is GROSS, overstates every defense ~15 yds/g and shifts the whole league one tier rung (silent + uniform); validated NET 331.6 yds/g for 2023 vs the NFL's published 331.1 (gross 349.0). (2) **the degenerate-baseline gate requiring BOTH MAE *and* RMSE is load-bearing** — `fum` won MAE 7/7 but LOST RMSE 7/7 because 67% of players fumble zero times = the NF-D11 conditional-median inversion firing on LIVE data. ⚠️ PUBLISHED CAVEAT (deliberately NOT gated on): the yards family's last 3 seasons are weak (ρ≈0.05 vs 0.27 full-sample), but that window was chosen AFTER seeing results → gating on it would be a post-hoc trim; re-validate after 2026 (carded — five consecutive weak seasons = regime change).
🧨 **THREE POST-MERGE DEFECTS, ALL THE SAME SHAPE — "a declaration that outran its production," each INVISIBLE to CI because every mechanism behaved exactly as designed (→ CLAUDE.md):** (a) **#575 — the module shipped UNINVOKED.** #573 wired the four player-side terms into the profile + editor catalog + both frontend mirrors + the exporter key list — and NOTHING ever called `project_captured_terms` → they'd export null and keep reporting "captured." **A term is APPLIED when something COMPUTES it, not when a column name appears in the profile/catalog/export map.** Cure: an `apply_to_projection` on the read path + a `CONSUMER_CALLERS` registry over all four loaders + an exhaustiveness guard. (b) **#577 — a stale cache published a graduated term ALL-NULL.** `load_team_defense_seasons` cached to `team_defense_{lo}_{hi}.parquet` keyed on the SEASON RANGE ALONE, so a parquet written 4 days before the SQL change kept returning the old column set (`proj_def_forced_fumble` 0/32 non-null). **Cure: a stale cache must be impossible to READ, not merely noisy — the key now carries a SHA of the QUERY TEXT.** ⚠️ found ONLY by reading the PUBLISHED ARTIFACT (not code/logs/CI), and the end-to-end verify of (a) MISSED (b) because it ran in a FRESH WORKTREE with no `artifacts/` cache → rebuilt clean + passed = the board-regen artifact-precedence landmine facing the SHIP direction (copy the real stale artifact in FIRST to validate an on-disk-artifact/cache fix). (c) a per-season summary filename `..._summary_<year>.json` slipped the existing nested `*_summary.json` ignore rule (minor).
📦 **BACKFILL (#578):** 2019–2025 projections landed to the lake (`--backfill-from/--to`, one Inputs + one band panel, ~35s); `proj_def_forced_fumble` now 32/32 across 2019–2026. Leakage verified EMPIRICALLY off the emitted diagnostics (each season's train window ends exactly at season−1). Fixed a latent `_REPORT_PATH` fixed-filename bug (a historical run would overwrite the committed report for the served season — forward season keeps the canonical name, others write beside it). ⚠️ INTERPRETATION TRAP recorded: the coverage/rank-signal numbers are panel-wide + IDENTICAL for every season in a range — seven repetitions of 0.8566 are NOT seven validations (now printed once, labelled).
⏭️ **OPEN ITEMS (PM):** (1) **NO purge/exclusion endpoint for import telemetry** — STEP 0 required hand-archiving the operator's QA imports before the frequency×|weight| ranking was trustworthy; future QA imports WILL re-pollute it (CARDED). (2) **Past-season BOARDS deliberately NOT published** — only projections were backfilled; publishing 2019–2025 boards makes them fetchable in the live api-cache = a DELIBERATE decision, not a side effect. (3) yards-allowed family **re-validation after the 2026 season** (CARDED). (4) ESPN long-TD ids 15/16, 35/36, 45/46 left UNMAPPED on purpose (40+ vs 50+ thresholds unverifiable from the payload — mapping a 50+ rule onto the 40+ column would pay on ~13% of TD passes where the league pays ~6%). 🧰 Session-hygiene (cost real time): `run_kdst_projection` takes `--projection-season` (not `--season`); `export_draft_board_json --publish` needs `--s3-bucket credence-prod-s3-api-cache` unless `$CACHE_BUCKET` is exported (its NF1.7 guard hard-errors rather than silently staging — correct); a `two_pt` change requires re-running `run_league_board` too or the board CSVs + the published payload disagree. ───── Original prompt follows.
🎯 WHY: the honest-coverage machinery says a term is CAPTURED when no projection column backs it. That is the right behaviour, but it is a TODO, not an end state — every captured term is a rule the user's league scores and our board ignores. The first real Sleeper import surfaced this concrete list (a 12-team half-PPR league): `yds_allow_0_100/100_199/200_299/300_349/400_449/450_499/500_549/550p` (+6 to −6), `pass_td_40p`/`rush_td_40p`/`rec_td_40p` (+2), `fumble_rec_td` (+6), `st_player_td` (+6), `pat_missed` (−1), `def_forced_fumble`, `st_ff`, `st_fum_rec`, `def_st_ff`, `def_st_fum_rec` (+1), `fum` (−1), `two_pt` (+2).
⚖️ **⚠️ THE DISCIPLINE THAT MAKES THIS HONEST RATHER THAN COSMETIC — "project it so it's applied" IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY AN IMPROVEMENT.** A term projected with NO skill still moves the board; it just moves it on noise while WEARING the "applied" label, which is strictly worse than an honest "captured" because the user now believes we modelled it. ⇒ **a term graduates from captured to applied ONLY if its projection beats a degenerate baseline on held-out data** (the NF-D11 degenerate-ceiling rule: score an all-league-average arm and require the real projection to beat it). A term whose best projection ties the constant baseline STAYS CAPTURED, and the story records why — that is a finding, not a failure.
🧹 **STEP 0 (BEFORE reading NF-C0d's ranking to prioritize) — PURGE THE QA NOISE.** NF-C0d shipped 2026-08-03 with the operator's own Preview-testing imports written as REAL rows into the same `fantasy-import-telemetry/{platform}/*.json` S3 prefix the admin ranking reads, and there is NO delete/purge endpoint. So the ranking is polluted with test imports until cleaned. FIRST: purge (or set aside) the operator's QA rows from `s3://<artifacts-bucket>/fantasy-import-telemetry/` — either `aws s3 rm` the known test-import objects, or read all rows and drop the ones whose `imported_at` falls in the 2026-08-01→03 Preview-testing window / match the test leagues — THEN trust the frequency×|weight| order. ⚠️ Treat the ranking as DIRECTIONAL regardless (thin, beta-bounded sample — NF-C0d flag). ⚠️ ESPN keys arrive as RAW numeric ids (label not sent), no cross-platform merging (`(platform,key)`-grouped → a concept split across platforms is under-counted → eyeball-merge), and manual NF-C0b leagues are invisible to this pipe — so the telemetry INFORMS priority, it does not dictate it.
🔬 DO, in priority order (frequency × |weight| × projectability; NF-C0d's telemetry re-ranks this — AFTER the STEP-0 purge):
  (1) ⭐ **DST YARDS-ALLOWED TIERS — the clear winner and the cheapest.** ✅ **CORROBORATED ACROSS PLATFORMS (2026-08-01):** a second real ESPN league (642070) scores the same ladder on nine rungs from **+5 down to −7** (statIds 128–136) — so this is not one Sleeper league's quirk but a rule two different platforms' users actually set, and it is the one family where the import is currently forced to say "saved, not applied" about a large weight. It is now the highest-value CAPTURED term we know of, on evidence rather than on the original single-league guess. It is the DIRECT structural analog of NF1.6's already-shipped points-allowed tier work: emit `yds_allow_g_<bucket>` as the **expected number of games** the defense lands in each bucket, so a per-game tier table scores a season as `Σ_bucket tier_points × expected_games` — LINEAR in those columns, i.e. the league's table applied EXACTLY rather than approximated (the same property that made `dst_pa_g_*` work). Highest |weight| on the list (+6 to −6) and a genuinely projectable team-level quantity.
  (2) **`two_pt`, `pat_missed`, `fumble_rec_td`, `st_player_td`, `def_forced_fumble`** — check FIRST whether these already exist upstream and are merely un-EXPORTED; a column we already compute and drop at export is a 10-minute fix, not a model.
  (3) **Long-TD bonuses (`pass_td_40p`/`rush_td_40p`/`rec_td_40p`)** — needs a distribution over TD length, not just a TD count. Tractable (historical TD-length distributions are stable per position) but genuinely a model → §0.5 applies.
  (4) **Special-teams splits (`st_ff`, `st_fum_rec`, `def_st_ff`, `def_st_fum_rec`) and `fum`** — LOWEST priority and the most likely honest NULLs: these are ~0–2 events per team-season and plausibly indistinguishable from noise. **Expect to keep them captured**, and say so with the degenerate-baseline evidence rather than by assertion.
🔗 DEPENDS: NF-C0d's telemetry re-orders this list from real user leagues rather than from one league's settings — start with (1), which is justified by weight alone, and let telemetry drive (2)–(4).
🔒 HONEST: every graduated term ships with the held-out evidence that its projection beats the degenerate baseline; every term left captured ships with the evidence that it did NOT. `best_alpha`-equivalent (a projection product, no edge claim).
GATE/AC: the DST yards-allowed tier columns projected + exported (linear-in-expected-games so a league table applies exactly), the already-computed-but-unexported terms wired through, each graduation justified against a degenerate baseline on held-out data, terms that fail it explicitly LEFT captured with the evidence; the import coverage panel shows the improvement; fast gate green. 🌿 GIT (do this FIRST, before touching any file): `git checkout dev && git pull && git checkout -b <story-branch>`; commit + push to THAT branch, hand back a PR → `dev`. ⛔ NEVER work on / commit to `dev`/`main` directly, ⛔ NEVER `git add -A`; re-check `git branch --show-current` before each commit (a parallel session shares the checkout); any `--publish`/`deploy.sh` is a POST-MERGE operator step. (CLAUDE.md 🌿.)

▶ NF-D19 — NFL RANKINGS-BOARD TIER DEFECT: the "T#" badge RE-FIRES after untiered (DST/K) rows (one tier reads as several same-numbered tiers) + the top tiers are tiny/singleton (reads as broken)   [App + light board-export · 🧭 SONNET · a served-board display + tiering-rule fix · operator-found 2026-08-02]   ✅ DONE 2026-08-02 (PR #489 + follow-up #492, both merged to dev → prod; frontend auto-deploys on push to main — no Lambda deploy)

✅ NF-D19 DONE 2026-08-02 — VERDICT
🔎 STEP-0 DIAGNOSIS CORRECTED THE STORY'S PREMISE: the tier column is NOT computed server-side in the board export (`export_draft_board_json.py`/`run_league_board.py`) — there is NO server-side tier field at all. Tiers are 100% CLIENT-SIDE, one shared TS function `assignTiers()` (+ wrapper `positionTierMap()`) in `frontend/lib/draft-optimizer.ts`, reused by the Rankings board, the player page, AND the live draft optimizer. ⇒ fixing the ONE shared function fixed every consuming surface at once, and the AC's "consistent across BOTH serving blobs" was AUTOMATICALLY satisfied — both blobs already share the same `vor`/`pts` fields (NF1.5b) and tiers are a pure client-side function of those. (A separate Python `assign_tiers` in `fantasy_engine/draft.py` is CLI-only, out of scope.)
🔬 FIX 1 — BADGE RE-FIRE (`rankings-board.tsx`): the "starts a new tier" check compared each row to the IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING row; DST/K carry tier null ("—"), so each untiered row reset the comparison and re-showed the next skill row's badge. Fixed to compare against the LAST TIERED row, skipping untiered interruptions.
🔬 FIX 2 — TIER SIZING (`draft-optimizer.ts`), shipped in TWO passes (the instructive part):
  • PR #489 first cut: a FLAT minimum tier size of 3 (gap-based tiers under 3 merge into a neighbor). Operator picked floor=3 from 3 surfaced options (flat-2 / flat-3 / percentile-target-count).
  • ⚠️ REGRESSION found in operator review: flat floor=3 OVER-corrected — full-PPR WR collapsed to just T1/T2. Root cause = the MIRROR problem the floor didn't touch: a long gradually-declining pool (deep startable WR) where no single gap ever clears the global mean+k·std threshold was ALREADY one giant undifferentiated tier; merging the tiny elite groups on top made the whole position read as two tiers.
  • PR #492 fix (operator asked for a min AND max, DATA-DRIVEN not fixed constants): `minSize = max(2, round(n·4%))`, `maxSize = max(minSize+1, round(n·15%))` — both scale to the pool size `n`, so a ~20-player TE pool and an ~84-player Overall pool each land in a sensible tier count (~7–25 tiers) instead of sharing one flat number. Plus a NEW second pass: any tier over `maxSize` splits RECURSIVELY at its own largest INTERNAL gaps (per-group relative threshold, not an arbitrary even slice); a split is skipped if either side would fall under `minSize` (min wins over max on conflict).
✅ VERIFIED: `tsc --noEmit` clean + standalone Node re-implementations of both algorithms against synthetic data shaped like the reported symptoms (this repo has NO JS test runner). #492 reproduced the EXACT regression (3 elite-cliff + 65-player smooth tail = full-PPR WR shape): old min-only code → one giant group; new min+max → 14 well-formed tiers, none under floor/over ceiling. Also checked Overall-, TE-, and degenerate-tiny-pool shapes. Changelog: #492 AMENDED #489's same-week entry in place (the intermediate over-correction never reached users) rather than layering a "fixed-then-refixed" narrative.
🧵 PROCESS: both PRs built off `dev` via a git WORKTREE (the session's tree was mid-task on an unrelated `mh2.1-wide-window-retrain` branch with uncommitted files) — worktree kept NF-D19 fully isolated. A clean example of the per-session-branch discipline flexing to an interrupted tree.
⚠️ NOT VERIFIED (the one open item): NO automated browser/UI check exists in this repo (no JS test runner wired at all) — everything was `tsc` + synthetic Node re-impl of the algorithm, NOT the real served board. OPEN: a live post-deploy spot-check of the Overall tab (ranks ~76–90 where DST/K interleave) + the full-PPR WR tab, against real data. → carded as the NF-D19 verify follow-up.
🔗 DURABLE LESSONS (→ sport_data_platform.md): (a) STEP-0 "where does this value come from" can INVERT a story's whole plan — a display concern assumed server-side was client-side, collapsing a two-blob-consistency AC to a one-function fix; (b) a symmetric sizing bug needs a symmetric fix — a MIN floor without a MAX ceiling fixes small-tier collapse and CREATES big-tier collapse; make both data-driven off pool size `n`; (c) when there's no UI test runner, reproduce the reported SHAPE synthetically and assert the fix on it — but say plainly that synthetic ≠ served, and leave a live spot-check open. best_alpha=0.
🎯 WHY: operator screenshots of the SERVED NFL Rankings board (Overall, Full-PPR 3-WR) show TWO tier problems that read as broken to a user: (1) ⭐ **the "T#" tier-START badge RE-FIRES after every untiered row** — DST/K rows carry tier "—", and the badge logic keys on "previous VISIBLE row's tier ≠ this row's" → after each DST/K interruption the next skill player re-shows the highlighted "T7" badge, so ONE tier 7 looks like FOUR separate "T7" tiers (OBSERVED: the T7 badge at ranks 76 / 80 / 85 / 89 with DST/K "—" rows between). (2) **the top tiers are tiny/singleton** — T1=Bijan (1), T2=Chase (1), T3≈3, T4≈2, T5≈1, T6≈… — a "tier" of ONE player reads as unhelpful/broken even when the value gap is real.
🧭 STEP 0 — DIAGNOSE which LAYER each problem is (do first): (a) confirm the "multiple T7" is a DISPLAY badge bug (the served data has ONE tier 7 across those rows) vs a MODEL numbering bug (the tiering EMITTED multiple tiers numbered 7) — inspect the served board JSON's tier column for ranks 76–90. (b) LOCATE the tiering algorithm — the board tier column is computed in the BOARD EXPORT (`export_draft_board_json.py` / `run_league_board.py`), NOT `season_projection.py`'s interval-calibration tier; the badge render is `frontend/components/fantasy/rankings-board.tsx`.
🔬 DO:
  (1) **DISPLAY FIX (`frontend/components/fantasy/rankings-board.tsx`):** the tier-start badge must key on the previous TIERED row (SKIP untiered "—" DST/K rows) → a tier is ONE contiguous badge regardless of interleaved DST/K; a tier number maps to exactly one visual group.
  (2) **TIERING FIX (board export — the tiny-top-tier problem):** rebalance the tiering so the top tiers aren't mostly singletons. ⚠️ **"what makes a good tier" is a PRODUCT JUDGMENT** — propose a concrete rule (a minimum tier size with singletons merged into the next tier / a gap-based break with a size floor / a target tier count) and STATE the trade-off (a real value gap between elite players is legitimate, but a UI tier of 1 is poor UX); if genuinely ambiguous, surface 2–3 options to the operator rather than guessing. ⚠️ this CHANGES the SERVED board's tier column → **the NF1.5b TWO-SERVING-BLOB rule applies: `board_*.json` (`run_league_board.py`) AND `projections.json` (`export_draft_board_json.py`) must carry the SAME tiers** (fixing one and not the other = the same player tiered differently on two pages). Runtime-gate (serving change) + changelog.
🚨 APP-TARGET: `frontend/` (Next.js) + the board export in the fantasy scripts; ⛔ never Streamlit. `best_alpha=0` (a display/tiering fix, no edge claim).
GATE/AC: the tier badge renders ONE contiguous group per tier (no re-fire across DST/K), verified on the Overall board incl. the K/DST-interleaved ranges; the tiering rebalanced so top tiers aren't singleton (per the chosen rule, product-confirmed if ambiguous), CONSISTENT across BOTH serving blobs; runtime-gate + changelog; CI green. 🌿 GIT (do this FIRST, before touching any file): `git checkout dev && git pull && git checkout -b <story-branch>`; commit + push to THAT branch, hand back a PR → `dev`. ⛔ NEVER work on / commit to `dev`/`main` directly, ⛔ NEVER `git add -A`; re-check `git branch --show-current` before each commit (a parallel session shares the checkout); any `--publish`/`deploy.sh` is a POST-MERGE operator step. (2) build ROSTER-FIRST on the CERTIFIED FRAME (**89,954 player-weeks 2016–2025 REG, 46.1% RETAINED zeros**; 3 zero classes — bye / inactive / dressed-no-stat — kept distinct) — ⛔ do NOT fit on `stats_player_week` (it silently DELETES the zero atom → trains on the conditional-on-playing population). (3) SELECT ON CRPS + score an all-zero DEGENERATE CEILING EVERY run (measure it, don't reason); coverage is a FLOOR, never a target — ⭐ MAE is INVERTED at QB/TE (conditional median 0.0 every season) so a pooled MAE is wrong for HALF the board (NF-D14). (4) NGS is a SPARSE OVERLAY (39.5% of targeted WR/TE weeks, 0% of zero rows) → carry a present/absent flag, ⛔ NEVER impute 0 (imputed-0 separation = "unmeasured⇒bad"). (5) ⛔ do NOT use markets, weather, depth-chart RANK, or game-day INACTIVE status AS FEATURES (weather/market enter only via NF-W0a forward capture once it accrues; depth-rank is blocked on the cross-era comparability check). (6) PIN the label version on EVERY result — leaderboards compare only within one version. **(7) ⭐ ERA-NORMALIZE the `pbp_participation`-derived opponent/defense legs (NF-W0c 2026-08-05): the provider/semantics were silently REPLACED at 2023** — `was_pressure` FALSE-filled post-2023 (`False` = "no pressure" OR "not charted"; true-share 0.29→0.15), `route` NULL→blank-string-fill (`'' ≠ charted`; vocab changed), `ngs_air_yards` DIED (0% from 2023), `defense_coverage_type` semantics shifted ⇒ treat 2016–2022 vs 2023+ as TWO ERAS (era indicator or restrict to 2023+); NEVER pool pressure/coverage rates across the boundary. ✅ `offense_players` (the certified dropback proxy the contract relies on) is 100% non-null every season and SURVIVES the switch. ⇒ the degenerate foil to BEAT on held-out WEEKS is "season÷games + matchup" (already the §0.5 null below); miss it and it's a recorded NULL, not served.
🎯 WHY: EVERYTHING in-season needs a PER-GAME/WEEKLY projection that DOES NOT EXIST today — next-N-game numbers (NF-C6 Phase 2), a weekly-updating ROS, roster-aware drop/add (NF-C4), start/sit. The entire NF1 stack is SEASON projections (one row per player-season); the codebase acknowledges the gap ("only relevant to a future weekly/game-level model — revisit when a weekly model exists"). This epic BUILDS that capability. ⭐ **the operator flagged it 2026-08-01 as a not-currently-planned prerequisite** surfaced by the My Teams page ask.
🔬 SCOPE (first slice): a weekly per-game fantasy-point projection per player — **distributional** (point + honest interval, the NF1.9 posture), conditioned on the WEEKLY signals a season projection ignores: **opponent/defense matchup** (reuse `defense_source`/`run_forward_defense`), usage/snap trend, **availability/injury** (the NF-D14/D15 work), bye, home/road, weather. Emit **RAW components** (the MVP-1 raw-line philosophy) so ANY league's scoring scores it. A **weekly-updating ROS = Σ remaining weekly projections.**
⚖️ **§0.5 BAKE-OFF (it SELECTS a predictive model): ≥3 candidate learner classes + a DIRECT-LEARNED FOIL — the foil is the DEGENERATE weekly baseline "season projection ÷ games remaining, spread by a matchup adjustment" (the honest null a real weekly model MUST beat).** Purged/embargoed CV OVER WEEKS (not seasons); deflation (PBO<0.2 / DSR≥0.95 / FDR + placebo); NF-D11 degenerate ceiling + oracle floor; per-position. ⚠️ **a weekly projection that does NOT beat season÷games+matchup on held-out weeks is NOT worth serving — hold it as a recorded NULL** (the NF-C0e "captured stays captured unless it beats the degenerate" discipline). CRPS not MAE on the zero-heavy weekly target (bye/inactive weeks; the NF-D11/NF-D14 MAE-inversion lesson).
⚠️ **A MEATY EPIC — likely spins into sub-stories** (weekly data assembly · the skill-position bake-off · the ROS aggregation · weekly K/DST). Scope the FIRST slice = the skill-position weekly point projection + its honest weekly baseline; card the rest from the result.
🔒 `best_alpha=0` (a fantasy projection product — NO betting/edge claim). §0.5.
GATE/AC: a weekly per-game DISTRIBUTIONAL projection (raw components + honest interval) that BEATS the degenerate weekly baseline (season÷games + matchup) on purged/embargoed held-out WEEKS under deflation, per position — else a recorded null; a weekly-updating ROS = Σ remaining weeks. Feeds NF-C6 Phase 2 + NF-C4 (drop/add) + start/sit. 🌿 GIT (do this FIRST, before touching any file): `git checkout dev && git pull && git checkout -b <story-branch>`; commit + push to THAT branch, hand back a PR → `dev`. ⛔ NEVER work on / commit to `dev`/`main` directly, ⛔ NEVER `git add -A`; re-check `git branch --show-current` before each commit (a parallel session shares the checkout); any `--publish`/`deploy.sh` is a POST-MERGE operator step. (CLAUDE.md 🌿.)
```

## NF-C0f — ESPN LEAGUE IMPORT VIA USER-MEDIATED PASTE   ✅ DONE 2026-08-01 (shipped as part of the NF-C0 close-out — see the NF-C0 DONE block for the full verdict: cookie-can't-be-echoed-in-a-body reasoning, the no-network paste adapter, the credential scrubber, identity-verified stat map, the size-wall prune, and the 4× "≥2 real payloads" payoff).

🎯 GOAL: give ESPN users — the largest platform, and the one NF-C0 left on the manual floor — a league import that takes about a minute, **without our ever holding an ESPN credential**. The user opens their league's settings URL in their own signed-in browser, copies the JSON response, and pastes it into the import page. We parse it into the same `LeagueConfig` every other adapter produces. **We never make a request to ESPN.**

📌 WHY THIS IS RED-LINE-CLEAN (read `docs/nf_c0_espn_access_probe.md` §3(d) before writing code): `espn_s2` is an HTTP **cookie** — the browser attaches it to the request and it is **never echoed into the response body**, so a paste of the body is *structurally incapable* of carrying the session credential. We hold data, not a key: nothing is re-fetchable, we cannot write to the league, and access ends when the tab closes. This is categorically different from the §3(c) cookie-handling path that NF-C0 refused, and the memo's table spells out all four counts.

⛔ **NEVER offer "paste your cookie instead" as a fallback** for a user who finds the JSON copy awkward. That is §3(c) in a costume, and the convenience gap is precisely the pressure that would produce it. If the paste flow is too hard, the answer is better UX, not a credential.

🛠️ SCOPE:
- **URL construction.** Take the league ID (from their league URL, exactly like the Sleeper flow) + season, build `https://lm-api-reads.fantasy.espn.com/apis/v3/games/ffl/seasons/{season}/segments/0/leagues/{id}?view=mSettings`, and render it as a **one-click copy link that opens in a new tab**. Do not make the user assemble a URL. SSRF-guard the id (`^[0-9]{1,24}$`) exactly as `sleeper.py` does.
- **Paste + parse.** A textarea (and a file-upload alternative for large payloads). Parse SERVER-side into the canonical layer so there is ONE parser — but have the client **pre-trim to the `settings` object** before POSTing so a fat payload doesn't hit the Lambda's ~6 MB synchronous request ceiling. Cap the accepted body and return a clear error rather than a truncated parse.
- **🚨 Runtime credential scrubber (MANDATORY, and it does not exist yet).** Reject any input matching `espn_s2` / `SWID=` / `Cookie:` / `Authorization:` BEFORE parsing; return a plain-language explanation ("that looks like it includes your ESPN sign-in — copy just the JSON response body instead"); **never log the raw paste.** The existing red-line lint is a SOURCE scan — this is its runtime twin. Test it with a real "Copy as cURL" string.
- **Member GUIDs.** ESPN member `id`s are SWID-format GUIDs — an identifier, not a secret, but they identify a real ESPN account. Drop them unless needed to map a team to the importing user; never log them.
- **Scoring translation.** ESPN keys scoring by **numeric `statId`**, not name — build the map by ID (the same discipline `yahoo.py` used to avoid paying QBs for defensive interceptions). Unmapped terms carry through VERBATIM so `resolve_scoring` classifies them CAPTURED rather than dropping them.
- **Roster slots** from `settings.rosterSettings.lineupSlotCounts` (ESPN slot IDs → our `RosterSlot`); detect SUPERFLEX from slot ELIGIBILITY, never the slot name (the `canonical.detect_superflex` rule).

🔗 REUSE, DO NOT FORK: the same `LeagueConfig` / `ScoringTranslation` / APPLIED-DERIVED-CAPTURED coverage machinery as Sleeper and Yahoo, the same preview-before-save flow, the same `fantasy_leagues` Dynamo map. A second config schema is a defect.

📊 THE CAPTURED-TERM QUESTION IS ALREADY ANSWERED: an ESPN rule we don't project surfaces as CAPTURED in the preview (never as a silent zero), and **NF-C0d** aggregates those across users so the backlog is driven by real leagues. No new telemetry needed here — just make sure ESPN terms flow into the same counter.

🧪 VALIDATION: ≥2 INDEPENDENTLY-SOURCED real ESPN payloads before trusting the stat-ID map (the NF-C0 lesson: 56 tests over one live league still missed a mapping bug that the operator's own league found immediately — fixtures inherit the author's assumptions about which variant a platform emits). A redacted fixture of each goes in the test suite.

⚠️ FRAGILITY, STATED HONESTLY: this endpoint is undocumented and unversioned; ESPN has already moved the read host once (`fantasy.espn.com` → `lm-api-reads.fantasy.espn.com`) with no notice. A shape change breaks the parse, not a credential — so fail LOUD and specific ("ESPN changed their format, we're on it") and keep the NF-C0b manual editor reachable as the floor. Pin the assumed shape in a fixture so a break is a red test, not a user report.

🔒 HONEST: the changelog must say plainly that this takes a copy-paste because ESPN publishes no import API, and that we ask for the data rather than the sign-in **on purpose**. No edge claims; projection product.

GATE/AC: an ESPN league imports end-to-end from a paste and round-trips byte-identical through `LeagueConfig`; the scrubber rejects a real "Copy as cURL" string and is unit-tested; no credential material is logged (assert it); ≥2 real payloads mapped; coverage panel shows APPLIED/DERIVED/CAPTURED including ESPN-only terms; the copy link is one click; fast gate green. Operator handoff + `git add` + changelog.

✅ **SHIPPED + MERGED TO MAIN + DEPLOYED 2026-08-01** (paste flow, coverage panel, scrubber, 3 real payloads mapped across 2 leagues and 2 seasons; frontend on Vercel, API via `infrastructure/lambda/deploy.sh`, operator-confirmed working end-to-end) — then **EXTENDED the same day to TEAMS + ROSTERS** on the operator's call. The one link now carries `mSettings&mTeam&mRoster` (ESPN takes repeated `view=` params), so rosters cost the user no extra paste.
- ⏳ **THE PRE-DRAFT CASE IS THE NORMAL CASE, NOT AN EDGE CASE (operator, 2026-08-01).** People import BEFORE drafting — that is when a draft tool is worth having — and an undrafted ESPN league returns its teams with EMPTY rosters. That must read as a successful import with a next step ("import again after your draft"), never as a partial failure. Both real fixtures are undrafted, so this is the path with real-payload backing. Generalises to every platform adapter: **an import surface's most common state is the empty one, and the empty state is where the copy has to be best.**
- 🪤 **A CREDENTIAL GUARD CAN BE A FALSE-REFUSAL LANDMINE — WIDENING THE READ CAN BREAK IT (the sharpest lesson here).** The scrubber matched a bare `\bSWID\b`; adding `mTeam` makes the payload carry `members[].id` as a SWID GUID, so had ESPN labelled that field with the literal word, **every honest import would have been rejected with a message accusing the user of pasting credentials** — a security guard producing a total outage that reads as the user's fault. Narrowed to the cookie-assignment form (`SWID=`). ⭐ **THE DECIDING ARGUMENT IS THE ASYMMETRY, and it generalises: narrowing costs nothing when the real artifact is caught several times over (`espn_s2` anywhere + two `Cookie:` patterns + this), while over-matching breaks the feature for 100% of users.** Distinguish the CREDENTIAL (`espn_s2` — match anywhere, any syntax) from an IDENTIFIER (a SWID GUID — cannot authenticate alone; match only in credential-bearing syntax). ⇒ **whenever you widen what a paste/upload endpoint reads, re-audit its input guards against the NEW payload's field names — a guard tuned to the old payload can reject the new one outright.**
- ⚖️ **EVIDENCE BAR SHOULD SCALE WITH BLAST RADIUS, and saying so beats pretending to one standard.** `_PRO_TEAM_BY_ID` ships at a deliberately LOWER bar than `SCORING_KEY_MAP`: a wrong stat id **silently misprices a league** (numbers the user acts on), a wrong pro-team abbreviation shows a **wrong badge beside a correct player** on a roster we display but never score. Unknown id → `None`, never a guess. Position is derived from `eligibleSlots` against the ALREADY-VERIFIED `ROSTER_SLOT_MAP` rather than ESPN's separate `defaultPositionId` table — **reuse verified evidence instead of importing a second unverified numbering.**
- ✅ **CLOSED — the populated-roster path is VALIDATED, via a PRIOR SEASON.** Both current-season leagues are `drafted: false`, so no obtainable payload contained a roster — but `previousSeasons` does: swapping `seasons/2026`→`seasons/2025` on the same link returned a fully drafted league. ⭐ **GENERALISABLE: a HISTORICAL read supplies a payload state the current season cannot produce** — reach for it wherever an integration's interesting state is seasonal. All four guessed field names confirmed; `_PRO_TEAM_BY_ID` now identity-checked on 7 rows against the players actually on those teams; all 7 players read with correct position, pro team and starter flag (incl. an IR player correctly counted as a non-starter).
- 🪤 **THE NEAR-MISS THAT JUSTIFIES THE WHOLE "REUSE VERIFIED EVIDENCE" RULE — two id spaces that overlap enough to look interchangeable.** ESPN's `defaultPositionId` is a **DIFFERENT numbering from lineup slots**: `defaultPositionId` 4 = **TE**, lineup **slot** 4 = **WR**. The obvious simplification — read the position id against the slot map — would have imported **Kittle and Andrews as WR and left Mahomes/Adams/McMillan with NO position at all**, silently, on a roster we display. Deriving from `eligibleSlots` against the ALREADY-VERIFIED map avoided it. ⇒ **when a payload offers two ways to get the same fact, prefer the one whose mapping you have already checked; a second numbering is a second thing to be wrong about.** Pinned by a test asserting the 4=WR/4=TE collision so it cannot be re-proposed from memory.
- 📦 **A TRUNCATED SAMPLE HIDES A SIZE WALL — the FULL file was a ship-blocker find.** The complete drafted response is **3.29 MB for a TEN-team league** against a 4 MB paste cap ⇒ **~99% at 12 teams, OVER the cap at 14** — the most common league sizes failing on payload size alone, with an unactionable "too large" message. **~96% of it is fields the import never reads** (`stats`, `draftRanksByRankType`, `ownership`, `ratings`, `notificationSettings`); the client now prunes before upload: **3.29 MB → 147 KB**. ⭐ **GENERALISABLE: an integration's real payload SIZE is a first-class contract, and a partial sample cannot reveal it — get one complete response before trusting a size limit.** Two design calls worth reusing: prune by **DENYLIST not allowlist** (client and API deploy independently, so an allowlist client would silently starve a newer server of a field it began reading — a denylist of verified-unread fields is safe in BOTH skew directions), and **pass a non-parsing paste through untouched** so a pruning bug can never turn a good paste into a rejected one, and a cURL paste still reaches the credential scrubber. Invariant asserted: pruning does not change the imported league.
- 🥅 **THE UNVALIDATED SHAPES WERE THE LAST ONES SAMPLED — validate the WHOLE population, not the first rows.** The visible portion of the truncated paste held only QB/RB/WR/TE; **K and D/ST appeared nowhere in it**, and a D/ST is not a person but a TEAM (`fullName` "Packers D/ST", a **NEGATIVE** player id) — the entry most likely to fall through handling written for skill players. The full file covers 172 entries across 10 teams: 0 missing positions, 0 missing pro teams. ⭐ **And it turned the pro-team table from inference into evidence for free: every D/ST NAMES ITS OWN CLUB and carries that club's id, so all 15 are self-identifying identity checks (15/15, plus all 32 ids exercised).** Look for a field that is self-describing — it converts a lookup table from asserted to proven at zero cost.
- 🔒 **A REAL FIXTURE MUST BE ANONYMISED BEFORE IT IS COMMITTED.** The payload carries leaguemates' first+last names, ESPN account GUIDs and per-member notification settings. Committed fixture preserves the GUID SHAPE (the parser and scrubber must handle it) and drops the identities; multi-KB per-player `stats` arrays trimmed as we never read them. **Validating against real data does not mean committing real people's data.**
- 📋 Also disclosed rather than papered over: we do NOT mark which team is the importing user (ESPN's response never says, and the credential that would is the one we refuse to hold), and member SWID GUIDs label a team then get dropped ("not a credential" is not a reason to keep one).
